[protobuf] Wire new-patch and fixup CLI and agent guide in README

`NewPatch`, `FixupPatch`, and fixup conflict recovery are implemented in
`roll_protobuf.py`, and the earlier milestones already expose `check`,
`plan`, `prepare`, `start`, `status`, `continue`, `drop`, `resume`,
`generate`, and `abort`.

Without CLI subcommands for `new-patch` and `fixup` or explicit
guardrails in `README.chromium`, human rollers and supervised AI coding
agents still lack CLI entry points for creating or amending patches and
clear rules on which actions require human approval.

Wire `new-patch <patch>` and `fixup <patch>` (plus dirty-worktree next
command ordering in `GetRollStatus`) into `roll_protobuf.py` CLI and
document `new-patch`, `fixup`, and "Using an agent:" in
`//third_party/protobuf/README.chromium`.

[protobuf] Support fixup rebase conflicts in status, abort, and guards

While `FixupPatch` and `ContinueRoll` can start and resume a conflicting
`fixup` rebase, `GetRollStatus` and `AbortRoll` still only inspect
`state.json`, and other mutating subcommands (`prepare`, `drop`,
`resume`, `start`, and `generate`) do not check for an in-progress git
rebase or stale `fixup.json`.

Running `status` or `abort` during a paused `fixup` rebase therefore
fails to report or roll back `pre_fixup_head` while preserving
`state.json`, and running another roll subcommand mid-rebase can corrupt
working-tree state.

Extend `GetRollStatus`, `FormatRollStatusReport`, and `AbortRoll` in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` to report and abort active
`fixup` rebases (`fixup.json`), restoring `pre_fixup_head` while
preserving any underlying `state.json`, and add active-rebase guards to
`PrepareRoll`, `DropCurrentPatch`, `ResumeRoll`, `StartRoll`, and
`GenerateBootstrapFiles`.

[protobuf] Add _ContinueFixupRebase for conflicting fixup rebases

When `roll_protobuf.py fixup` stops on a downstream rebase conflict, it
persists `fixup.json` and instructs the user to resolve the conflict and
run `roll_protobuf.py continue`, but `ContinueRoll` currently only
handles `state.json` `conflict` state from `start` or `resume`.

Without `_ContinueFixupRebase` wired into `ContinueRoll`, users cannot
resume a conflicting `fixup` rebase, step through multi-commit rebase
conflicts, or automatically rewrite `patches/*.patch` once the rebase
completes.

Add `_ContinueFixupRebase` and wire `ContinueRoll` in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` to validate conflict
resolution and scope hygiene across patch and non-patch rebase steps,
roll back to `pre_fixup_head` if a resolved patch becomes empty, and
finalize `patches/*.patch` when the rebase finishes.

[protobuf] Add unmerged-index and rebase-matching helpers for fixup

`_LoadActiveFixupState` currently checks only whether a git rebase
directory exists alongside `fixup.json`, and
`_FindFilesWithConflictMarkers` only detects textual `<<<<<<<` conflict
markers in working-tree files.

If a user aborts the `fixup` rebase and starts an unrelated `git rebase`
(or hits a modify/delete or binary conflict that leaves unmerged index
stages without textual conflict markers), `roll_protobuf.py` could
misidentify the active rebase or allow `continue` to stage unresolved
files.

Add `_IsMatchingFixupRebase`, `_CommitTouchedPaths`, and
`_FindUnresolvedUnmergedPaths` in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` to verify `onto` and
`orig-head~1` against `FixupState`, sync live `(index, total)` rebase
counters, and compare working-tree blob OIDs against index stages 2 and
3 to catch markerless unmerged files.

[protobuf] Add FixupPatch for roll_protobuf.py fixup

Amending an earlier `"Apply patches/NNNN-*.patch"` commit on a roll
branch currently requires manually creating a `fixup!` commit, running
`git rebase -i --autosquash`, and regenerating every downstream
`patches/*.patch` file whose line numbers or context shifted.

Performing that rebase by hand risks leaving `patches/*.patch` files or
`state.json` `expected_head` out of sync with the rewritten commits, or
leaving an untracked git rebase state if a downstream patch conflicts.

Add `FixupResult`, `_FinalizeCompletedFixup`, `FixupPatch`, and
`FormatFixupReport` in `//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` to fold
uncommitted `src/` and `python/` edits into an earlier patch commit via
non-interactive `git rebase --interactive --autosquash`, rewrite
`patches/*.patch` along the branch, and persist `fixup.json` with
recovery instructions when a rebase conflict occurs.

[protobuf] Add FixupState and target-patch lookup for fixup

`roll_protobuf.py` currently persists roll progress in `state.json`
(`GITDIR/protobuf-roll/state.json`), but has no state model for tracking
an in-progress `fixup` interactive rebase or resolving a user-supplied
patch identifier to its `"Apply patches/NNNN-*.patch"` commit on the
current branch.

Without persistent `fixup.json` metadata and target-commit lookup
helpers, `roll_protobuf.py fixup` cannot validate that a target patch is
applied once and not dropped later on the branch, or recover cleanly
when `git rebase --interactive --autosquash` stops on a conflict.

Add `FixupState`, `_SaveFixupState`, `_RemoveFixupState`,
`LoadFixupState`, `_LoadActiveFixupState`, `_IsGitRebaseInProgress`,
`_RebaseGitEnvironment`, `_ResolveFixupRebaseBase`,
`_FindTargetPatchCommit`, and `_BuildFixupConflictState` in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` with unit tests in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf_test.py`.

[protobuf] Add _RewritePatchFilesAlongBranch for roll_protobuf.py fixup

When an earlier `Apply patches/NNNN-*.patch` commit on a roll branch is
amended via `git rebase --autosquash`, downstream patch files whose line
numbers or context shifted become stale, and any rebased manual
`"Regenerate bootstrap files"` commit loses its link to the pre-rebase
generator inputs.

Leaving shifted `.patch` files unrefreshed causes `roll_protobuf.py
check` or the next upstream roll to fail, and losing the pre-rebase
generator digest hides stale bootstrap files when an amended patch
touches compiler sources.

Add `_RewritePatchFilesAlongBranch` in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` to rebuild `patches/*.patch`
blobs in commit order via `_UpdatePatchesSubtree` without checking out
intermediate commits, stamp pre-rebase `Bootstrap-Inputs-Hash` trailers
onto manual regeneration commits when generator inputs change, and sync
the refreshed `.patch` files to disk and `.git/index`.

[protobuf] Add git tree and bootstrap-digest helpers for fixup

`roll_protobuf.py` currently computes bootstrap generator input digests
from the live working tree (`_ComputeBootstrapInputsDigest`) and has no
helpers for reading or updating `third_party/protobuf/patches/` subtrees
directly in the git object database.

To rewrite `patches/*.patch` files across a branch after a `fixup`
rebase without checking out intermediate commits or losing the link
between an amended generator patch and a downstream `"Regenerate
bootstrap files"` commit, `fixup` needs in-memory git tree manipulation
and commit-tree bootstrap digest helpers.

Add `_APPLY_PATCH_SUBJECT_PATTERN`, `_DROP_PATCH_SUBJECT_PATTERN`,
`_ListGitTree`, `_ReadPatchesSubtreeBlobs`, `_UpdatePatchesSubtree`,
`_ComputeCommitBootstrapDigest`, `_FindUnstampedRegenTarget`, and
`_PatchFileTouchesGenerator` in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf.py` with unit tests in
`//third_party/protobuf/roll_protobuf_test.py`.

3576bbb0e1a6aff8e2d60c120b612eaff9982736 [ diff ] 
[tvOS] Filter out DestroySurfaceEmbedChildAfterEmbedderProcessDied

The test kills the embedder's renderer with content::CrashTab(), but
tvOS runs in single-process mode, where RenderProcessHost::Shutdown()
returns false without doing anything. The renderer stays alive, so the
crash expectations in CrashTab() and the embedder frame node check that
follows it all fail.

Bug: 436306678

1d3439ea5035e6e95a54218cfbf3dd7f14b6a50d [ diff ] 
[CAA] Show last uploaded signals report in connectors-internals

The signals JSON on chrome://connectors-internals/#signals-reporting
is generated on demand when the page is opened, without a challenge
from DMServer. Client certificates are only collected when a challenge
is available (each certificate signal signs it), so the page always
showed "certificates_count": 0 even when the reports actually uploaded
by ReportScheduler contained certificates. This made the page
misleading when debugging certificate collection. I also refactored the
last security report config field.

Tested-locally: https://screenshot-v2.corp.google.com/image/0doc3plfruktg.png

Bug: 502633960

[Views] Enable transposed scroll test in `WidgetScrollViewTest`

`CompositedTransposedScrollEventsHorizontalComponentIsLarger` in
`WidgetScrollViewTest` was disabled in https://crrev.com/c/4104727.

Git history for `ui/views/controls/scroll_view.cc` was investigated:
`ScrollView::Layout` and `ScrollView::SetControlVisibility` were updated
(https://crrev.com/c/7417269, https://crrev.com/c/8372429, https://crrev.com/c/8618740) so transposed scroll
events deterministically update horizontal scroll offsets, though the
exact repairing commit could not be pinpointed. Re-enable the test.
Verified on Linux.

Part of http://go/arthurmilchior-fix-all-test-skill-chrome for AI week.

Mark Performance.getEntries* with Affects=Nothing
📋 Metadata

3cbf602a923ab6fae52c6534fdbad08a470cd3cc
[ log ]
author

tree
58ddd0bffff8773e97d294e1ede13977367028c0
parent
31a875e8aa6310fc4ffcbdf130c33336eb49530c [ diff ] 
Mark Performance.getEntries* with Affects=Nothing

- Context: DevTools Console eager evaluation and the DevTools AI
  executeJavaScript tool evaluate expressions with throwOnSideEffect:
  true. Because performance.getEntries(), getEntriesByType(), and
  getEntriesByName() lacked [Affects=Nothing], calling them aborted with
  EvalError: Possible side-effect in debug-evaluate.
- Why this was missed: Blink IDL attributes default to
  kHasNoSideEffect, whereas IDL methods default to kHasSideEffect unless
  explicitly annotated with [Affects=Nothing]. These three methods only
  read buffered PerformanceEntry records without mutating state.
- Prior art: performance.now() in the same interface is already marked
  [Affects=Nothing], and performance.timeOrigin, performance.timing, and
  performance.navigation are side-effect-free readonly attributes.

tree
079d81c8ece96799ac128f4679b0718b37c18209
parent
f9be67ea27dd7ff06caca6f20019a19fda9be05c [ diff ] 
[Scroll Timing] Make shared buffer payload access lock-free

Replace the 32-byte atomic_ref<ScrollTimingInfo> with per-field
SharedAtomic values, which enforce lock-free access at compile time.
This avoids process-local locking fallbacks while preserving the
existing seqlock ordering and overwrite detection.

Remove the explicit padding and whole-record shared-memory assertions,
which are no longer needed for field-by-field copies. Destructure the
record in Push() so adding fields requires updating the transport.

Follow-up to the atomic_ref review comments in crrev.com/c/8478674.

[GPU] Re-enable `PointCoordTest.RenderTo` on Linux and ChromeOS

`PointCoordTest.RenderTo` was disabled in release builds on Linux (and
later ChromeOS via https://crrev.com/c/2351281) due to flakiness on an
old Linux ATI bot (https://crbug.com/40299409).

Git history was investigated and the exact repairing commit could not be
pinpointed, though `gl_tests` now runs on ANGLE/SwANGLE and modern Mesa
drivers. Re-enable `PointCoordTest.RenderTo`. Verified on Linux.

Part of http://go/arthurmilchior-fix-all-test-skill-chrome for AI week.

[Cronet] Add HTTP flag to disable setIdempotency calls

Introduce the Cronet_disable_set_idempotency HTTP flag. When enabled,
calls to ExperimentalUrlRequest.Builder#setIdempotency are silently
ignored so that the request retains DEFAULT_IDEMPOTENCY.

7936eba5d756b05a7032cd933c15e055346a5b9a [ diff ] 
[Cronet] Add HTTP flag to disable shared Brotli dictionaries

Introduce the Cronet_disable_shared_brotli HTTP flag. When enabled,
calls to UrlRequest.Builder#setRawCompressionDictionary are silently
ignored as a no-op (including skipping argument validation) so that
compression dictionaries are not attached to requests.

8504ec5b2757a105bdca5e7c0dac25be4b377831 [ diff ] 
Use base::EnumSet and base::span for CryptAuth feature types

Replaces base::flat_set with base::EnumSet (as CryptAuthFeatureTypeSet)
for CryptAuth feature types, allowing the sets to be constructed and
passed efficiently by value without requiring heap allocation or
base::NoDestructor.

Also updates GetAllCryptAuthFeatureTypeStrings() to return a
base::span<const std::string_view> instead of a base::flat_set, and
updates call sites and unit tests to match.

tree
c45f6cceef922df56a52c4d16f70b9d372f33d02
parent
5af8a22d27140cba77356f8888ca7403ce6022a1 [ diff ] 
[Cronet] Add HTTP flag to disable Network Quality Estimator

Add the `Cronet_disable_nqe` boolean HTTP flag to
`CronetUrlRequestContext`. When enabled, Network Quality Estimator (NQE)
is not enabled in the native `URLRequestContextConfig`, and NQE calls on
`CronetUrlRequestContext` (`getEffectiveConnectionType`, `getHttpRttMs`,
`getTransportRttMs`, `getDownstreamThroughputKbps`,
`configureNetworkQualityEstimatorForTesting`, `addRttListener`,
`removeRttListener`, `addThroughputListener`,
`removeThroughputListener`) behave as no-ops or return unknown metric
constants without throwing `IllegalStateException`.

[Contextual cue] Set allow_credentials=false in MES prewarming

When prewarming the MES connection, ContextualCueingService currently
sets allow_credentials=true.

Since ModelExecutionFetcherImpl omits credentials, the preconnected
socket is placed into a different socket pool partition and is never
reused.

Code traces:
- allow_credentials maps to network::mojom::CredentialsMode
- network::mojom::CredentialsMode to net::PrivacyMode (see e.g.
  NetworkContext::PreconnectSockets)
- The sockets are keyed by ClientSocketPool::GroupId, which contains
  net::PrivacyMode as a member.

This CL adds the kGlicZeroStateSuggestionsPreconnectWithoutCredentials
feature (disabled by default) to allow experimenting with omitting
credentials during preconnection.

Bug: 579793514

98560c6c2b13e83790f7554b7cb9664b99b3feeb
parent
ba22dd0d9436c5115c21ee58e6b9a240bc9fec44 [ diff ] 
[CSD] Lift browser-side visual classification into the host base class

Move the browser-process classification pipeline (classifier and image
embedder ownership, the retained classification image, completion
handling and Scorer-change cancellation) from ClientSideDetectionHostIOS
into ClientSideDetectionHostBase so that content platforms can reuse it
once they capture page pixels in the browser (crbug.com/454896977).
Platforms supply CaptureScreenshot() and
DetermineVisualFeaturesExtraction().

This is a pure move: iOS behavior is unchanged and the new code is
unreachable on Desktop/Android, where ClientSideDetectionHost still
drives the renderer over Mojo. The one addition is that a Scorer change
now also marks CSD idle, since the dropped request never reports back.

Bug: b:454896977
